# Canonical Evolved AOSE — neutral audit intake

Revision `EAOSE-CANONICAL-R1-2026-09-07`. Population 132/132 examined; no target has been inspected. Counts and evidence partitions are those in the canonical property/source tables. Questions below are conditional enquiries, not universal controls or assertions that a target has a gap.

Before using any row, establish the actual subject, consequential failure and trigger. Accept outcomes **already addressed**, **inapplicable**, **simpler mechanism sufficient**, **evidence insufficient**, or **scoped improvement justified**. Do not infer adoption from relevance. In particular, preserve conventional or non-agent implementation when it satisfies the same needs with lower justified cost.

Intention, obligation, authority, permission, capability, issued action, executed action, observed effect and accepted outcome are different states. The inquiry must not use one as evidence of all the others. Changes may invalidate applicability/support without logically falsifying the earlier conclusion; method revision does not authorise itself.

## EAOSE-C001 — Affected-stakeholder validation

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Affected-stakeholder validation. A challenged actor inventory and acceptance criteria expose omissions and unresolved disagreement.

**Mature mechanism:** Elicit and challenge the set of affected actors, including people who do not operate or purchase the system; validate goals with those actors or explicitly qualified representatives.

**Controlled failure:** A complete diagram can omit an affected non-user; consultation can be tokenistic.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** Several parties can be helped, harmed or made dependent by the system. / For a genuinely single-party local task, a short agreed requirement and counterexample review can replace an actor diagram.

**Prerequisites and authority:** {"DATA": "Actual affected actors and their accounts; proposed goals/dependencies, constraints, legitimacy and observable acceptance information.", "PROPERTY_DEPENDENCIES": [], "SPECIFIC_PRECONDITIONS": "Access to affected parties or a disclosed representation limit; a boundary that can be revised."} Requirements engineer and affected participants; the sponsor cannot silently speak for all parties.

**Evidence and consumer:** {"OBSERVABLE_POSTCONDITION": "A challenged actor inventory and acceptance criteria expose omissions and unresolved disagreement.", "CANONICAL_CRITERION": "A challenged actor inventory and acceptance criteria expose omissions and unresolved disagreement.", "NEGATIVE_CANDIDATE_NOT_AN_ACTIVE_CONTROL": false} Consumer: Requirements engineer and affected participants; the sponsor cannot silently speak for all parties.. Communication: Communicate the decision-relevant result to its actual consumer: Requirements engineer and affected participants; the sponsor cannot silently speak for all parties. The operative evidence is: A challenged actor inventory and acceptance criteria expose omissions and unresolved disagreement. This is not a requirement for a new message protocol when an existing shared representation suffices..

**Criticism and evidence ceiling:** {"CRITICISM_IDS": ["C01"], "SOURCE_IDS": ["EAOSE-CS044", "EAOSE-CS056"], "RESEARCHER_OBJECTION": "A complete diagram can omit an affected non-user; consultation can be tokenistic."} All source support is inherited at the exact frozen claim/access scope except explicitly linked FV records. Retention is a conditional engineering judgement, not proof of comparative effect or target adequacy.

**Omission / anti-ceremony boundary:** Omit separate machinery when the stated trigger is absent or a cheaper demonstrated substitute preserves this exact criterion. For a genuinely single-party local task, a short agreed requirement and counterexample review can replace an actor diagram.

**Neutral questions:** Does the actual case exhibit this row's trigger and controlled failure? What concrete observation would show that the criterion is already met, and who is legitimately able to use that evidence? Could the cheaper path preserve the same criterion? Which source assumptions, authority boundaries or costs are absent or different? Is the row being misused as a universal obligation despite its disposition?

**References:** sources EAOSE-CS007, EAOSE-CS011, EAOSE-CS016, EAOSE-CS026, EAOSE-CS031, EAOSE-CS032, EAOSE-CS044, EAOSE-CS048, EAOSE-CS056; criticisms EAOSE-CC01; tensions EAOSE-CT01; ancestry A:EAOSE-001, A:EAOSE-042, B:EAOSE-001. ## EAOSE-C002 — Stakeholder-goal to observable-acceptance correspondence

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Stakeholder-goal to observable-acceptance correspondence. The acceptance condition tests the intended stakeholder outcome, including prohibited outcomes, rather than merely an internal goal flag or terminated action.

**Mature mechanism:** Translate selected stakeholder objectives into observable acceptance and prohibited-outcome conditions; challenge AND/OR refinements as hypotheses about sufficiency.

**Controlled failure:** Good measurable proxies may omit consent, harm or long-term effects; goal satisfaction can be gamed.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** A system is to be accepted as satisfying a stakeholder goal. / A small executable example plus a human acceptance decision may suffice; not every concern needs a formal goal tree.

**Prerequisites and authority:** {"DATA": "Actual affected actors and their accounts; proposed goals/dependencies, constraints, legitimacy and observable acceptance information.", "PROPERTY_DEPENDENCIES": ["EAOSE-C001"], "SPECIFIC_PRECONDITIONS": "A declared observer, relevant environment and criterion; stakeholder authority for trade-offs."} Stakeholders author/approve the acceptance meaning; testers produce evidence; implementers cannot redefine success after a failure.

**Evidence and consumer:** {"OBSERVABLE_POSTCONDITION": "Accepted evidence distinguishes a completed local action from achievement of the intended goal.", "CANONICAL_CRITERION": "The acceptance condition tests the intended stakeholder outcome, including prohibited outcomes, rather than merely an internal goal flag or terminated action.", "NEGATIVE_CANDIDATE_NOT_AN_ACTIVE_CONTROL": false} Consumer: Stakeholders author/approve the acceptance meaning; testers produce evidence; implementers cannot redefine success after a failure.. The operative evidence is: Accepted evidence distinguishes a completed local action from achievement of the intended goal. This is not a requirement for a new message protocol when an existing shared representation suffices..

## EAOSE-C004 — Dependency vulnerability and accountability

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Dependency vulnerability and accountability. A dependency failure can be traced to affected goals and an authorised response, rather than an unexplained broken edge.

**Mature mechanism:** Describe each material dependence by depender, dependee, needed result/resource and exposure if it is not delivered; connect failure to a decision or fallback.

**Controlled failure:** Dependency pictures can be mistaken for enforceable commitments or universal accountability.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** Requirements depend on another actor's cooperation, availability or resources. / An ordinary interface contract is sufficient when it names the real dependency and its failure response.

## EAOSE-C005 — Goal conflict and alternative analysis

**Disposition / examination:** USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Goal conflict and alternative analysis. The selected option and reasons change intelligibly when a material preference or feasibility premise changes.

**Mature mechanism:** Expose conflicting goals and compare feasible alternatives under explicit priorities, hard constraints and uncertainty; revise choices when those assumptions change.

**Controlled failure:** Uncalibrated softgoal links can masquerade as quantitative evidence; hidden weights predetermine the outcome.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** Different actors or qualities cannot all be maximised together. / Direct stakeholder choice is cheaper when a single transparent trade-off suffices.

## EAOSE-C006 — Delegation distinguished from authority

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Delegation distinguished from authority. An action or obligation can be linked to valid standing, scope and revocation state.

**Mature mechanism:** Separate a request, an assignment, a commitment and an authorisation; check who can create, transfer or revoke obligations and operational powers.

**Controlled failure:** Mental intention, capability and normative obligation can each be mistaken for permission.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** One actor acts or promises on behalf of another. / A direct approved call inside one trust boundary may avoid a delegation protocol while retaining permission checks.

## EAOSE-C007 — Effective capability and permission checks

**Disposition / examination:** STRONGLY_RETAINED / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Effective capability and permission checks. An incapable or unauthorised actor is not silently treated as a valid fulfiller.

**Mature mechanism:** Check both the practical ability and current permission to perform a consequential operation at the point of use.

**Controlled failure:** Design-time permissions may be stale; valid credentials do not prove competence or successful effects.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** A role or agent may be allocated work it cannot or may no longer perform. / A conventional access-control and capability check may suffice; no specialised agent registry is required.

## EAOSE-C009 — Non-functional constraints and adverse consequences

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Non-functional constraints and adverse consequences. A successful task is accepted only under the applicable consequence and quality constraints, with unresolved trade-offs exposed.

**Mature mechanism:** Treat latency, resource use, safety, privacy, security and adverse stakeholder consequences as requirements with explicit acceptance and conflict rules, not as optional additions to task success.

**Controlled failure:** Proxy metrics omit affected parties; averaging can hide catastrophic failures. A norm-aware planner's willingness to violate a soft norm does not authorise violating a hard access boundary.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** Task achievement can cause material cost or harm, or service qualities determine acceptability. / A narrow low-risk computation can use a small explicit quality budget rather than a full assurance programme.

## EAOSE-C010 — Modular requirements views and explicit interfaces

**Disposition / examination:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A stakeholder/requirements judgement and the actors, dependencies or acceptance statements it concerns: Modular requirements views and explicit interfaces. Users can reason locally without losing consequential inter-view dependencies.

**Mature mechanism:** Partition large requirements models into views with explicit interfaces and cross-view consistency responsibilities.

## EAOSE-C048 — Goal completion checked against consequences

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** An agent design, internal decision process, environment interface or executable realisation: Goal completion checked against consequences. Goal lifecycle transitions reflect the declared success condition, not merely exhausted steps.

## EAOSE-C050 — Belief provenance, revision and observational uncertainty

**Bearer and criterion:** An agent design, internal decision process, environment interface or executable realisation: Belief provenance, revision and observational uncertainty. ## EAOSE-C051 — Intention retention and bounded reconsideration

**Disposition / examination:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** An agent design, internal decision process, environment interface or executable realisation: Intention retention and bounded reconsideration. The system can explain why pursuit continued, was suspended or was abandoned, and what happened to dependent obligations.

**Mature mechanism:** Retain an adopted intention long enough to coordinate useful action, with explicit reconsideration when achievement, impossibility, irrelevance or competing obligations warrant it; account for the cost of reconsideration itself.

## EAOSE-C057 — Resource budgets and termination conditions

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** An agent design, internal decision process, environment interface or executable realisation: Resource budgets and termination conditions. Termination has a recorded reason and reconciles unresolved work rather than confusing stopped execution with achieved goals.

**Mature mechanism:** Bound computation, messages, tokens, retries, elapsed time or other relevant resources and define what termination means for goals, obligations and already-issued effects.

**Controlled failure:** Token limits may depend on reported usage; checks after a response cannot prevent all overshoot. Stopping a conversation does not cancel remote effects.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** Autonomous or conversational loops can continue indefinitely or consume unacceptable resources. / A statically bounded operation may need only ordinary timeout and cost limits.

**Mature mechanism:** Validate the mappings from environmental observations to beliefs and from selected actions to external effects, separately from the correctness of deliberation.

**Controlled failure:** Ground beliefs may be wrong; successful tool return can occur without the intended external consequence.

## EAOSE-C093 — Exhaustive testing guarantees unrestricted autonomous behaviour

**Disposition / examination:** REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** no; adverse, contested, unresolved or superseded candidate.

## EAOSE-C103 — Selective premise-sensitive revalidation of assurance and requirements

## EAOSE-C111 — Accountability and evidence custody

**Disposition / examination:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM / EXAMINED. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** An operational system state, transition, change or evidence handoff: Accountability and evidence custody. A relevant action can be attributed and its supporting evidence inspected or explicitly reported unavailable without silent alteration.

**Mature mechanism:** Preserve attributable evidence of consequential decisions, communications and effects for the parties entitled to examine them, with integrity, access and retention boundaries proportionate to the stakes.

**Controlled failure:** Incomplete logs, unrecorded reasoning, forged narratives and over-retention undermine accountability or privacy. Auditability is not proof that the decision was right.

## EAOSE-C129 — Generated plans remain subject to executable constraints

**Disposition / examination:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A bounded learned/symbolic agent-engineering component or modern translation: Generated plans remain subject to executable constraints. Plausible natural-language plans are not treated as executable specifications or accepted proofs.

**Mature mechanism:** Require generated candidate plans to meet the selected language semantics, applicability and authority constraints before execution; distinguish formal strategy synthesis from language-model suggestion.

**Controlled failure:** Validation can check only the encoded constraints; learned grounding and environmental action models can remain wrong.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** A planner or learned model generates or revises executable behaviour. / Keep a verified finite plan library or require manual selection when generation offers no justified benefit.

## EAOSE-C130 — Preserve responsiveness during long-running plan generation

**Disposition / examination:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT / EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT. **Active mechanism in this intake:** conditional.

**Bearer and criterion:** A bounded learned/symbolic agent-engineering component or modern translation: Preserve responsiveness during long-running plan generation. Waiting for a plan does not by itself disable every unrelated reactive obligation; the wait has an explicit failure or resumption path.

**Mature mechanism:** Suspend the intention waiting for generated plans rather than the whole agent reasoning loop, where the runtime supports this; preserve the ability to handle unrelated events while a slow or failed generation call is outstanding.

**Controlled failure:** Non-blocking scheduling does not ensure hard real-time response, resource fairness or semantic validity of a late result; cancellation and stale-state handling still need design.

**Trigger / cheaper path:** Remote or computationally slow plan generation threatens the response time of other responsibilities assigned to the same agent. / Use a prewritten plan or synchronous execution when no concurrent response obligation exists and the delay is acceptable.
